<qwenmeasurement20261101:doc xmlns:qwenmeasurement20261101="http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0">
# Retain the Qwen3.8 local-model trial

For maintainers comparing this downloaded model with the earlier CPU pilot. The report states exactly which settings are held fixed and keeps reconstruction, held-out behavior and prose quality separate. Raw records preserve failures as well as accepted constructions.

<qwenmeasurement20261101:entry path="docs/developers/local-agent-qwen38-2026-11-01.md">
# Qwen3.8-27B on the local CPU — 1 November 2026

For maintainers deciding whether the newly downloaded model is usable on the 32 GiB Ryzen 5 3600 machine. This is a small feasibility comparison, not a general model ranking.

Removed about 50.3 GB of rebuildable Rust caches: the ignored `hickory-docs-literate/target/debug` directory (release build retained), and `pilockdb-literate/target/debug/incremental` (binaries and dependencies retained). Tracked files were preserved. Downloaded `qwen3.8:27b` through Ollama. The model manifest and identity are retained in the evidence.

The document loop uses the same three tasks, instructions, evaluator version and budgets as the earlier Qwen3-Coder pilot: CPU execution, six threads, 8192-token context, 1536-token output cap, temperature 0.2, top_p 0.95, top_k 20, seed 42 and thinking disabled. A short readiness probe loads the model before fixture timing.

| Task | Model | Completed | Held-out checks | Iterations | Output tokens | Decode t/s | Wall time |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| intervals | Qwen3-Coder 30B | True | True | 2 | 804 | 11.5 | 77.2 s |
| intervals | Qwen3.8 27B | True | True | 2 | 1280 | 3.8 | 535.6 s |
| ledger | Qwen3-Coder 30B | True | True | 2 | 484 | 11.9 | 44.5 s |
| ledger | Qwen3.8 27B | True | True | 2 | 551 | 3.2 | 340.3 s |
| tokens | Qwen3-Coder 30B | True | True | 2 | 666 | 11.6 | 60.3 s |
| tokens | Qwen3.8 27B | True | True | 2 | 1292 | 3.7 | 550.5 s |

Fixed prompts and budgets do not force equal reply lengths. Decode throughput divides reported output tokens by Ollama generation time; wall time also includes prompt processing and evaluation. Inspect both token volume and phase timing before attributing a wall-time difference to the model alone.

The accepted `intervals` and `tokens` documents were byte-identical to their initial drafts. The fixed polishing condition still requested a second pass. These wall times therefore include an extra revision/verification request even when it changed nothing; they are not optimized one-pass latency figures. First-call durations are retained in the raw evidence.

Candidate programs run in fresh Monty namespaces for each test case, with no host callbacks. Hickory reconstructs their exact output bytes. The polishing pass freezes the initial passing Python AST and reconstructs final source again. This preserves executable structure during polishing; it does not promise unchanged initial source bytes or independently prove explanation accuracy. Public checks guide repair; held-out cases are evaluated only after stopping.

- [intervals accepted document](../experiments/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38/intervals/project.md).
- [ledger accepted document](../experiments/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38/ledger/project.md).
- [tokens accepted document](../experiments/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38/tokens/project.md).

## Tool-and-submission fixture

One ledger run uses the existing action loop with read, candidate evaluation, status and literate-submission tools: focused instructions, 8192-token context, 768-token output cap, temperature 0.2 and a twelve-turn limit. This is an isolated development fixture, not the application approval flow.

| Model | Completed | Saved candidates | Held-out checks | Wall time |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Qwen3-Coder 30B, earlier stage trial | False | 0 | n/a | 259.2 s |
| Qwen3.8 27B | True | 1 | True | 572.8 s |

Actual handoffs recorded in the Qwen3.8 run: 1. Enabling handoff alone does not establish that one occurred. The earlier Qwen3-Coder stage trial failed before acceptance; its latency overlapped model loading and compilation. Timing is descriptive, not an isolated speedup estimate.

All seven saved candidate programs were separately reconstructed and regraded after the live trials. They passed their public and held-out cases in fresh interpreter namespaces, and the initial/final AST fingerprints match for each polishing pair.

The sample contains one run per task and model, and its simple functions do not establish long-horizon coding reliability, production tool use or parity with Fable 5. The document loop remains an isolated prototype. See the [earlier report](local-agent-results-2026-10-31.md) for the separate action-loop and context-placement limitations.

[Raw requests, replies, timings, candidates and receipts](../experiments/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38/records.json) include the download manifest, model digest and readiness probe. Reproduce with the [study guide](local-agent-study.md), substituting `--model qwen3.8:27b` and a new output directory.
</qwenmeasurement20261101:entry>

      "text": "{\n  \"model\": \"qwen3.8:27b\",\n  \"settings\": {\n    \"context_tokens\": 8192,\n    \"output_tokens\": 1536,\n    \"temperature\": 0.2,\n    \"top_p\": 0.95,\n    \"top_k\": 20,\n    \"threads\": 6,\n    \"think\": false,\n    \"seed\": 42,\n    \"response_format\": \"text\",\n    \"timeout_seconds\": 900\n  },\n  \"tasks\": [\n    \"intervals\",\n    \"ledger\",\n    \"tokens\"\n  ],\n  \"max_iterations\": 6,\n  \"polish\": true,\n  \"instruction_sha256\": \"16884e3ab9917184a23070e6c8f189b76188e6b2d25f8fba3de7a0efbb244872\",\n  \"evaluator_version\": \"ast-polish-v4-fresh-cases-heading\",\n  \"scope\": \"Document-as-state feasibility pilot; not a controlled replacement for live agent UI\"\n}\n"

    "2026-11-01-qwen38-document/intervals/accepted.md": {
      "sha256": "0b1098b06451ce254ecef9e063395ca043edd4283f625ac241be18af69a55e67",
      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# Merging Overlapping Half-Open Intervals\n\nThis document presents a solution to merge overlapping half-open intervals. The core challenge is to correctly identify overlaps while respecting the half-open nature of the intervals, where touching endpoints do not constitute an overlap.\n\n## Construction\n\nThe algorithm proceeds in three main steps:\n\n1.  **Immutability and Sorting**: First, we create a deep copy of the input list to ensure we do not mutate the original data. We then sort these copied intervals by their start time. Sorting is a prerequisite for the linear merge pass.\n\n2.  **Linear Merge Pass**: We iterate through the sorted intervals. We maintain a `result` list.\n    *   If the `result` list is empty, we append the first interval.\n    *   Otherwise, we compare the current interval's start time with the end time of the last interval in the `result` list.\n    *   **Overlap Condition**: Two half-open intervals `[a, b)` and `[c, d)` overlap if and only if `c < b`. If `c >= b`, they are either disjoint or merely touching (in the case `c == b`).\n    *   If they overlap (`current_start < last_end`), we merge them by updating the end time of the last interval in `result` to be the maximum of the two end times.\n    *   If they do not overlap (`current_start >= last_end`), we append the current interval to `result`.\n\n3.  **Return**: Finally, we return the `result` list.\n\nThis approach ensures that touching intervals (e.g., `[1, 3]` and `[3, 5]`) are kept separate, while overlapping intervals (e.g., `[1, 4]` and `[2, 6]`) are correctly merged into `[1, 6]`.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    if not data:\n        return []\n    \n    # Create a copy to avoid mutating the input\n    intervals = [list(interval) for interval in data]\n    \n    # Sort by start time\n    intervals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])\n    \n    result = [intervals[0]]\n    \n    for i in range(1, len(intervals)):\n        current_start, current_end = intervals[i]\n        last_start, last_end = result[-1]\n        \n        # Overlap condition for half-open intervals: current_start < last_end\n        if current_start < last_end:\n            # Merge: update the end of the last interval in result\n            result[-1][1] = max(last_end, current_end)\n        else:\n            # No overlap (disjoint or touching), append current interval\n            result.append([current_start, current_end])\n            \n    return result\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"
    },

We maintain a `result` list.\n    *   If the `result` list is empty, we append the first interval.\n    *   Otherwise, we compare the current interval's start time with the end time of the last interval in the `result` list.\n    *   **Overlap Condition**: Two half-open intervals `[a, b)` and `[c, d)` overlap if and only if `c < b`. If `c >= b`, they are either disjoint or merely touching (in the case `c == b`).\n    *   If they overlap (`current_start < last_end`), we merge them by updating the end time of the last interval in `result` to be the maximum of the two end times.\n    *   If they do not overlap (`current_start >= last_end`), we append the current interval to `result`.\n\n3.  **Return**: Finally, we return the `result` list.\n\nThis approach ensures that touching intervals (e.g., `[1, 3]` and `[3, 5]`) are kept separate, while overlapping intervals (e.g., `[1, 4]` and `[2, 6]`) are correctly merged into `[1, 6]`.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    if not data:\n        return []\n    \n    # Create a copy to avoid mutating the input\n    intervals = [list(interval) for interval in data]\n    \n    # Sort by start time\n    intervals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])\n    \n    result = [intervals[0]]\n    \n    for i in range(1, len(intervals)):\n        current_start, current_end = intervals[i]\n        last_start, last_end = result[-1]\n        \n        # Overlap condition for half-open intervals: current_start < last_end\n        if current_start < last_end:\n            # Merge: update the end of the last interval in result\n            result[-1][1] = max(last_end, current_end)\n        else:\n            # No overlap (disjoint or touching), append current interval\n            result.append([current_start, current_end])\n            \n    return result\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"

      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# Merging Overlapping Half-Open Intervals\n\nThis document presents a solution to merge overlapping half-open intervals. The core challenge is to correctly identify overlaps while respecting the half-open nature of the intervals, where touching endpoints do not constitute an overlap.\n\n## Construction\n\nThe algorithm proceeds in three main steps:\n\n1.  **Immutability and Sorting**: First, we create a deep copy of the input list to ensure we do not mutate the original data. We then sort these copied intervals by their start time. Sorting is a prerequisite for the linear merge pass.\n\n2.  **Linear Merge Pass**: We iterate through the sorted intervals. We maintain a `result` list.\n    *   If the `result` list is empty, we append the first interval.\n    *   Otherwise, we compare the current interval's start time with the end time of the last interval in the `result` list.\n    *   **Overlap Condition**: Two half-open intervals `[a, b)` and `[c, d)` overlap if and only if `c < b`. If `c >= b`, they are either disjoint or merely touching (in the case `c == b`).\n    *   If they overlap (`current_start < last_end`), we merge them by updating the end time of the last interval in `result` to be the maximum of the two end times.\n    *   If they do not overlap (`current_start >= last_end`), we append the current interval to `result`.\n\n3.  **Return**: Finally, we return the `result` list.\n\nThis approach ensures that touching intervals (e.g., `[1, 3]` and `[3, 5]`) are kept separate, while overlapping intervals (e.g., `[1, 4]` and `[2, 6]`) are correctly merged into `[1, 6]`.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    if not data:\n        return []\n    \n    # Create a copy to avoid mutating the input\n    intervals = [list(interval) for interval in data]\n    \n    # Sort by start time\n    intervals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])\n    \n    result = [intervals[0]]\n    \n    for i in range(1, len(intervals)):\n        current_start, current_end = intervals[i]\n        last_start, last_end = result[-1]\n        \n        # Overlap condition for half-open intervals: current_start < last_end\n        if current_start < last_end:\n            # Merge: update the end of the last interval in result\n            result[-1][1] = max(last_end, current_end)\n        else:\n            # No overlap (disjoint or touching), append current interval\n            result.append([current_start, current_end])\n            \n    return result\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"
    },

    "2026-11-01-qwen38-document/ledger/accepted.md": {
      "sha256": "8dcedfe60cb8f52c10e72622aceed8a2a6e8a3886bbc9f9acb498285a1309ed8",
      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# In-Order Balance Processing\n\nThe task requires processing a list of signed integer entries against a starting nonnegative balance while preserving the original entry order. The key constraint is that an entry must be rejected if applying it would make the balance negative; rejected entries do not change the balance, and processing continues with subsequent entries. This ordering rule is essential: sorting deposits first would alter which entries are rejected and is therefore incorrect.\n\nThe chosen construction is a single linear pass over `entries`. For each entry, the prospective balance is computed as the current balance plus the entry value. If that prospective balance is negative, the zero-based index is recorded in the rejected list and the balance is left unchanged. Otherwise, the entry is applied to the balance. The final result is a dictionary containing the final balance and the list of rejected indices.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    balance = data[\"balance\"]\n    entries = data[\"entries\"]\n    rejected = []\n    for i, e in enumerate(entries):\n        if balance + e < 0:\n            rejected.append(i)\n        else:\n            balance += e\n    return {\"balance\": balance, \"rejected\": rejected}\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"
    },

      "text": "{\n  \"accepted\": true,\n  \"scope\": \"isolated development candidate; no production review\",\n  \"source_output_equal\": true,\n  \"public_checks\": {\n    \"passed\": true,\n    \"cases\": [\n      {\n        \"passed\": true,\n        \"actual\": {\n          \"balance\": 3,\n          \"rejected\": [\n            0\n          ]\n        },\n        \"expected\": {\n          \"balance\": 3,\n          \"rejected\": [\n            0\n          ]\n        },\n        \"input_unchanged\": true\n      },\n      {\n        \"passed\": true,\n        \"actual\": {\n          \"balance\": 2,\n          \"rejected\": [\n            1\n          ]\n        },\n        \"expected\": {\n          \"balance\": 2,\n          \"rejected\": [\n            1\n          ]\n        },\n        \"input_unchanged\": true\n      }\n    ]\n  },\n  \"output_hash\": \"74b5388b2be8238dd4da30d628c3905a490a0a1d79d282a44bee52b1dd543c8e\",\n  \"algorithm_guard\": \"identical Python AST across polishing\"\n}\n"
    },
    "2026-11-01-qwen38-document/ledger/candidate-1.md": {
      "sha256": "df185340ce23a04fab66d29ade8e9948d13cbac5a1ec8ef6db818dfc49a06eeb",
      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# In-Order Balance Processing\n\nThe required behavior is straightforward: walk through `entries` in their original order, applying each signed integer to the running balance. If an entry would push the balance below zero, that entry is rejected and its zero-based index is recorded; the balance stays unchanged and processing continues with the next entry. This preserves the original sequence, which is the key correction over the earlier “deposits first” approach.\n\nThe implementation below is pure Python with no imports. It initializes the balance from the input, iterates with `enumerate` to track indices, and appends rejected indices only when the prospective balance would be negative.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    balance = data[\"balance\"]\n    entries = data[\"entries\"]\n    rejected = []\n    for i, e in enumerate(entries):\n        if balance + e < 0:\n            rejected.append(i)\n        else:\n            balance += e\n    return {\"balance\": balance, \"rejected\": rejected}\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"
    },

    "2026-11-01-qwen38-document/ledger/candidate-2.md": {

      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# In-Order Balance Processing\n\nThe task requires processing a list of signed integer entries against a starting nonnegative balance while preserving the original entry order. The key constraint is that an entry must be rejected if applying it would make the balance negative; rejected entries do not change the balance, and processing continues with subsequent entries. This ordering rule is essential: sorting deposits first would alter which entries are rejected and is therefore incorrect.\n\nThe chosen construction is a single linear pass over `entries`. For each entry, the prospective balance is computed as the current balance plus the entry value. If that prospective balance is negative, the zero-based index is recorded in the rejected list and the balance is left unchanged. Otherwise, the entry is applied to the balance. The final result is a dictionary containing the final balance and the list of rejected indices.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    balance = data[\"balance\"]\n    entries = data[\"entries\"]\n    rejected = []\n    for i, e in enumerate(entries):\n        if balance + e < 0:\n            rejected.append(i)\n        else:\n            balance += e\n    return {\"balance\": balance, \"rejected\": rejected}\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"

    "2026-11-01-qwen38-document/tokens/accepted.md": {
      "sha256": "0c2a372c045cf3615ee534a23d95ac48ce543adc6c96d04dce4457a776386c8f",
      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# Whitespace Token Counter in First-Appearance Order\n\nThis document specifies a small pure-Python helper that counts whitespace-separated tokens while preserving their first appearance order. The construction is intentionally dependency-free: it relies only on built-in string and list operations, so it can run in restricted environments without imports.\n\n## Construction\n\n1. **Normalize the input.** If `data` is `None` or not a string, treat it as empty. This keeps the function total and avoids raising on odd inputs.\n2. **Split on whitespace.** `str.split()` with no argument splits on any run of whitespace and drops leading/trailing whitespace, which matches the required behavior for empty or whitespace-only input.\n3. **Lowercase each token.** `str.lower()` is applied to each token after splitting. Punctuation is preserved because lowercasing only affects case-sensitive letters.\n4. **Count in first-appearance order.** A plain dictionary is used as an ordered counter. Python dictionaries preserve insertion order, so the first time a token is seen it is inserted, and later occurrences only increment its count. This avoids the earlier mistake of sorting alphabetically.\n5. **Return the required shape.** Convert the ordered dictionary into a list of two-element lists: `[[token, count], ...]`.\n\n## Why this satisfies the samples\n\n- `\"B a B\"` splits to `[\"B\", \"a\", \"B\"]`, lowercases to `[\"b\", \"a\", \"b\"]`, and counts to `{\"b\": 2, \"a\": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `b` then `a`, giving `[[\"b\", 2], [\"a\", 1]]`.\n- `\" Hi! hi HI! \"` splits to `[\"Hi!\", \"hi\", \"HI!\"]`, lowercases to `[\"hi!\", \"hi\", \"hi!\"]`, and counts to `{\"hi!\": 2, \"hi\": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `hi!` then `hi`, giving `[[\"hi!\", 2], [\"hi\", 1]]`.\n\n## Edge cases\n\n- Empty string `\"\"` → `[]`.\n- Whitespace-only string `\"   \"` → `[]`.\n- Single token `\"Hello\"` → `[[\"hello\", 1]]`.\n- Mixed punctuation `\"a,a a,a\"` → `[[\"a,a\", 2]]` because punctuation is preserved inside tokens.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    if not isinstance(data, str):\n        return []\n    tokens = data.split()\n    counts = {}\n    for token in tokens:\n        key = token.lower()\n        if key in counts:\n            counts[key] += 1\n        else:\n            counts[key] = 1\n    return [[token, count] for token, count in counts.items()]\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"
    },

      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# Whitespace Token Counter in First-Appearance Order\n\nThis document specifies a small pure-Python helper that counts whitespace-separated tokens while preserving their first appearance order. The construction is intentionally dependency-free: it relies only on built-in string and list operations, so it can run in restricted environments without imports.\n\n## Construction\n\n1. **Normalize the input.** If `data` is `None` or not a string, treat it as empty. This keeps the function total and avoids raising on odd inputs.\n2. **Split on whitespace.** `str.split()` with no argument splits on any run of whitespace and drops leading/trailing whitespace, which matches the required behavior for empty or whitespace-only input.\n3. **Lowercase each token.** `str.lower()` is applied to each token after splitting. Punctuation is preserved because lowercasing only affects case-sensitive letters.\n4. **Count in first-appearance order.** A plain dictionary is used as an ordered counter. Python dictionaries preserve insertion order, so the first time a token is seen it is inserted, and later occurrences only increment its count. This avoids the earlier mistake of sorting alphabetically.\n5. **Return the required shape.** Convert the ordered dictionary into a list of two-element lists: `[[token, count], ...]`.\n\n## Why this satisfies the samples\n\n- `\"B a B\"` splits to `[\"B\", \"a\", \"B\"]`, lowercases to `[\"b\", \"a\", \"b\"]`, and counts to `{\"b\": 2, \"a\": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `b` then `a`, giving `[[\"b\", 2], [\"a\", 1]]`.\n- `\" Hi! hi HI! \"` splits to `[\"Hi!\", \"hi\", \"HI!\"]`, lowercases to `[\"hi!\", \"hi\", \"hi!\"]`, and counts to `{\"hi!\": 2, \"hi\": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `hi!` then `hi`, giving `[[\"hi!\", 2], [\"hi\", 1]]`.\n\n## Edge cases\n\n- Empty string `\"\"` → `[]`.\n- Whitespace-only string `\"   \"` → `[]`.\n- Single token `\"Hello\"` → `[[\"hello\", 1]]`.\n- Mixed punctuation `\"a,a a,a\"` → `[[\"a,a\", 2]]` because punctuation is preserved inside tokens.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    if not isinstance(data, str):\n        return []\n    tokens = data.split()\n    counts = {}\n    for token in tokens:\n        key = token.lower()\n        if key in counts:\n            counts[key] += 1\n        else:\n            counts[key] = 1\n    return [[token, count] for token, count in counts.items()]\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"

      "text": "<hick:doc xmlns:hick=\"http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0\">\n\n# Whitespace Token Counter in First-Appearance Order\n\nThis document specifies a small pure-Python helper that counts whitespace-separated tokens while preserving their first appearance order. The construction is intentionally dependency-free: it relies only on built-in string and list operations, so it can run in restricted environments without imports.\n\n## Construction\n\n1. **Normalize the input.** If `data` is `None` or not a string, treat it as empty. This keeps the function total and avoids raising on odd inputs.\n2. **Split on whitespace.** `str.split()` with no argument splits on any run of whitespace and drops leading/trailing whitespace, which matches the required behavior for empty or whitespace-only input.\n3. **Lowercase each token.** `str.lower()` is applied to each token after splitting. Punctuation is preserved because lowercasing only affects case-sensitive letters.\n4. **Count in first-appearance order.** A plain dictionary is used as an ordered counter. Python dictionaries preserve insertion order, so the first time a token is seen it is inserted, and later occurrences only increment its count. This avoids the earlier mistake of sorting alphabetically.\n5. **Return the required shape.** Convert the ordered dictionary into a list of two-element lists: `[[token, count], ...]`.\n\n## Why this satisfies the samples\n\n- `\"B a B\"` splits to `[\"B\", \"a\", \"B\"]`, lowercases to `[\"b\", \"a\", \"b\"]`, and counts to `{\"b\": 2, \"a\": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `b` then `a`, giving `[[\"b\", 2], [\"a\", 1]]`.\n- `\" Hi! hi HI! \"` splits to `[\"Hi!\", \"hi\", \"HI!\"]`, lowercases to `[\"hi!\", \"hi\", \"hi!\"]`, and counts to `{\"hi!\": 2, \"hi\": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `hi!` then `hi`, giving `[[\"hi!\", 2], [\"hi\", 1]]`.\n\n## Edge cases\n\n- Empty string `\"\"` → `[]`.\n- Whitespace-only string `\"   \"` → `[]`.\n- Single token `\"Hello\"` → `[[\"hello\", 1]]`.\n- Mixed punctuation `\"a,a a,a\"` → `[[\"a,a\", 2]]` because punctuation is preserved inside tokens.\n\n<hick:project/>\n\n<hick:file path=\"solution.py\">\ndef solve(data):\n    if not isinstance(data, str):\n        return []\n    tokens = data.split()\n    counts = {}\n    for token in tokens:\n        key = token.lower()\n        if key in counts:\n            counts[key] += 1\n        else:\n            counts[key] = 1\n    return [[token, count] for token, count in counts.items()]\n</hick:file>\n\n</hick:doc>"
    },

    "work/qwen38_action_pipeline.py": {
      "sha256": "163584e9aafc0393862f5d22b67dcbfa60c8ce3c4993e2c12aa41f516115631a",
      "text": "from pathlib import Path\nimport json,os,subprocess,time\nroot=Path.cwd();base=root/'target/local-agent';status=base/'qwen38-action-status.json';previous=base/'qwen38-status.json'\ndef state(phase,**data):status.write_text(json.dumps(dict(phase=phase,utc=time.strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ',time.gmtime()),**data),indent=2)+'\\n')\nstate('waiting for document pilot; inference stays sequential');deadline=time.monotonic()+14400\nwhile True:\n if previous.exists():\n  try:row=json.loads(previous.read_text())\n  except ValueError:row={}\n  if row.get('phase')=='document pilot finished':break\n  if any(word in row.get('phase','') for word in ['failed','timed out','exhausted']):state('skipped because document pipeline did not finish',previous=row);raise SystemExit(1)\n if time.monotonic()>deadline:state('wait budget exhausted');raise SystemExit(1)\n time.sleep(5)\nstate('running ledger tool-and-submission protocol fixture')\nargs=[str(root/'apps/agent/.venv/bin/python'),'-m','hickory_agent_worker.local_experiment','--model','qwen3.8:27b','--hick','target/debug/hick','--output','target/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38-action','--tasks','ledger','--temperatures','0.2','--instruction-profile','focused','--max-turns','12','--modes','handoff']\nwith (base/'qwen38-action.log').open('w') as output:\n try:result=subprocess.run(args,env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONPATH=str(root/'apps/agent/src')),stdout=output,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,timeout=1800)\n except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:state('action fixture time budget exhausted; partial records retained');raise SystemExit(1)\nstate('action pilot finished',exit_code=result.returncode)\nprint(status.read_text(),flush=True)\n"
    },

    "work/qwen38_pipeline.py": {
      "sha256": "16a0c85c136042722c3a4e70170c7e9674b0456f852446cf026dd887f5a215e7",
      "text": "from pathlib import Path\nimport os,json,subprocess,time\nroot=Path.cwd();base=root/'target/local-agent';status=base/'qwen38-status.json'\npython=str(root/'apps/agent/.venv/bin/python');env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONPATH=str(root/'apps/agent/src'))\ndef state(phase,**data):status.write_text(json.dumps(dict(phase=phase,utc=time.strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ',time.gmtime()),**data),indent=2)+'\\n')\nmanifest=Path('/usr/share/ollama/.ollama/models/manifests/registry.ollama.ai/library/qwen3.8/27b')\nstate('waiting for verified model download');deadline=time.monotonic()+7200\nwhile not manifest.exists():\n if time.monotonic()>deadline:state('download wait timed out');raise SystemExit(1)\n time.sleep(5)\nstate('loading model and probing inference')\nprobe='''from hickory_agent_worker.local import OllamaModel,LocalSettings\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json,time\np=Path('target/local-agent/qwen38-probe.json')\ntry:\n model=OllamaModel('qwen3.8:27b',LocalSettings(output_tokens=32,response_format='text'))\n response=model.complete([{'role':'user','content':'Reply with exactly READY and no other text.'}])\n row={'identity':model.identity,'response':response.text,'finish_reason':response.finish_reason,'metrics':response.metrics}\n p.write_text(json.dumps(row,indent=2)+'\\\\n')\n if response.text.strip()!='READY':raise ValueError('Probe did not return READY; inspect raw response')\nexcept Exception as exc:\n error={'error':str(exc)}\n if p.exists():error['raw_probe']=json.loads(p.read_text())\n p.write_text(json.dumps(error,indent=2)+'\\\\n')\n raise\n'''\nwith (base/'qwen38-probe.log').open('w') as output:\n result=subprocess.run([python,'-c',probe],env=env,stdout=output,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)\nif result.returncode:state('inference probe failed',exit_code=result.returncode);raise SystemExit(result.returncode)\nstate('running three document construction and polishing fixtures')\nargs=[python,'-m','hickory_agent_worker.document_experiment','--model','qwen3.8:27b','--hick','target/debug/hick','--output','target/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38-document','--polish']\nwith (base/'qwen38-document.log').open('w') as output:\n try:result=subprocess.run(args,env=env,stdout=output,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,timeout=7200)\n except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:state('fixture time budget exhausted; partial journals retained');raise SystemExit(1)\nstate('document pilot finished',exit_code=result.returncode)\nprint(json.dumps(json.loads(status.read_text())),flush=True)\n"
    },
    "work/qwen38_regrade.py": {
      "sha256": "85e688f3339cc27f8dcbfd81eaeac739c918d1da4d279fd6f1784e7bd2247e51",
      "text": "from pathlib import Path\nimport ast,json,re,subprocess,tempfile,hashlib\nfrom hickory_agent_worker.local_experiment import TASKS,evaluate\nfrom hickory_agent_worker.hosted import monty,LIMITS\nroot=Path.cwd();base=root/'target/local-agent';tasks={t['id']:t for t in TASKS};rows=[]\nfor run in sorted(base.glob('2026-11-01-qwen38-*')):\n if not run.is_dir():continue\n for path in sorted(run.glob('*/candidate-*')):\n  if path.suffix not in ('.json','.md') or path.name.endswith('-receipt.json'):continue\n  task=next((t for name,t in tasks.items() if path.parent.name.startswith(name)),None)\n  if not task:continue\n  row=dict(path=str(path.relative_to(base)))\n  try:\n   expected=None\n   if path.suffix=='.json':\n    obj=json.loads(path.read_text());expected=obj['code'];document=obj['document']\n   else:document=path.read_text()\n   with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='hickory-regrade-') as scratch:\n    folder=Path(scratch);(folder/'project.md').write_text(document)\n    def hick(operation):\n     result=subprocess.run([str(root/'target/debug/hick'),'project',operation,'project.md','--root',scratch],capture_output=True,text=True,timeout=30)\n     if result.returncode:raise ValueError(result.stderr[-2000:])\n     return result.stdout\n    inventory=hick('generate');match=re.fullmatch(r'File ordinary (\\d+) bytes solution.py\\n?',inventory)\n    if not match or int(match[1])>12000:raise ValueError('Expected one bounded solution.py')\n    hick('materialize');hick('verify');code=(folder/'solution.py').read_text()\n    if expected is not None and code!=expected:raise ValueError('Reconstructed source differs from saved candidate')\n   tree=ast.dump(ast.parse(code),include_attributes=False)\n   with monty() as pool:\n    with pool.checkout(limits=LIMITS) as session:\n     public=evaluate(session,code,task['public']);hidden=evaluate(session,code,task['hidden'])\n   row.update(code_sha256=hashlib.sha256(code.encode()).hexdigest(),ast_sha256=hashlib.sha256(tree.encode()).hexdigest(),source_output_equal=True,public=public,hidden=hidden)\n  except Exception as exc:row['error']=str(exc)\n  rows.append(row)\nresult=dict(method='Independent offline reconstruction plus regrade in fresh Monty namespace per test case; never shown to model',rows=rows)\n(base/'qwen38-regrade.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,indent=2)+'\\n')\nprint('Regraded',len(rows),'saved candidates; public passes',sum(r.get('public',{}).get('passed',False) for r in rows),'held-out passes',sum(r.get('hidden',{}).get('passed',False) for r in rows))\n"
    }
  },
  "limits": [
    "Three small fixtures, one seed per model; no general model ranking or frontier parity claim",
    "No blinded explanation-quality assessment",
    "Same prompts and settings do not imply optimal sampling for either model",
    "CPU timing depends on model architecture, cache state and memory bandwidth; no GPU test",
    "Document and action pilots use isolated development fixtures; they do not validate production edit-review flows"
  ]
}

<qwenmeasurement20261101:entry path="docs/experiments/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38/intervals/project.md">
<hick:doc xmlns:hick="http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0">

# Merging Overlapping Half-Open Intervals

This document presents a solution to merge overlapping half-open intervals. The core challenge is to correctly identify overlaps while respecting the half-open nature of the intervals, where touching endpoints do not constitute an overlap.

## Construction

The algorithm proceeds in three main steps:

1.  **Immutability and Sorting**: First, we create a deep copy of the input list to ensure we do not mutate the original data. We then sort these copied intervals by their start time. Sorting is a prerequisite for the linear merge pass.

2.  **Linear Merge Pass**: We iterate through the sorted intervals. We maintain a `result` list.
    *   If the `result` list is empty, we append the first interval.
    *   Otherwise, we compare the current interval's start time with the end time of the last interval in the `result` list.
    *   **Overlap Condition**: Two half-open intervals `[a, b)` and `[c, d)` overlap if and only if `c < b`. If `c >= b`, they are either disjoint or merely touching (in the case `c == b`).
    *   If they overlap (`current_start < last_end`), we merge them by updating the end time of the last interval in `result` to be the maximum of the two end times.
    *   If they do not overlap (`current_start >= last_end`), we append the current interval to `result`.

3.  **Return**: Finally, we return the `result` list.

This approach ensures that touching intervals (e.g., `[1, 3]` and `[3, 5]`) are kept separate, while overlapping intervals (e.g., `[1, 4]` and `[2, 6]`) are correctly merged into `[1, 6]`.

<hick:project/>

<hick:file path="solution.py">
def solve(data):
    if not data:
        return []
    
    # Create a copy to avoid mutating the input
    intervals = [list(interval) for interval in data]
    
    # Sort by start time
    intervals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
    
    result = [intervals[0]]
    
    for i in range(1, len(intervals)):
        current_start, current_end = intervals[i]
        last_start, last_end = result[-1]
        
        # Overlap condition for half-open intervals: current_start < last_end
        if current_start < last_end:
            # Merge: update the end of the last interval in result
            result[-1][1] = max(last_end, current_end)
        else:
            # No overlap (disjoint or touching), append current interval
            result.append([current_start, current_end])
            
    return result
</hick:file>

</hick:doc></qwenmeasurement20261101:entry>

<qwenmeasurement20261101:entry path="docs/experiments/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38/ledger/project.md">
<hick:doc xmlns:hick="http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0">

# In-Order Balance Processing

The task requires processing a list of signed integer entries against a starting nonnegative balance while preserving the original entry order. The key constraint is that an entry must be rejected if applying it would make the balance negative; rejected entries do not change the balance, and processing continues with subsequent entries. This ordering rule is essential: sorting deposits first would alter which entries are rejected and is therefore incorrect.

The chosen construction is a single linear pass over `entries`. For each entry, the prospective balance is computed as the current balance plus the entry value. If that prospective balance is negative, the zero-based index is recorded in the rejected list and the balance is left unchanged. Otherwise, the entry is applied to the balance. The final result is a dictionary containing the final balance and the list of rejected indices.

<hick:project/>

<hick:file path="solution.py">
def solve(data):
    balance = data["balance"]
    entries = data["entries"]
    rejected = []
    for i, e in enumerate(entries):
        if balance + e < 0:
            rejected.append(i)
        else:
            balance += e
    return {"balance": balance, "rejected": rejected}
</hick:file>

</hick:doc></qwenmeasurement20261101:entry>

<qwenmeasurement20261101:entry path="docs/experiments/local-agent/2026-11-01-qwen38/tokens/project.md">
<hick:doc xmlns:hick="http://www.hickorydocs.com/1.0">

# Whitespace Token Counter in First-Appearance Order

This document specifies a small pure-Python helper that counts whitespace-separated tokens while preserving their first appearance order. The construction is intentionally dependency-free: it relies only on built-in string and list operations, so it can run in restricted environments without imports.

## Construction

1. **Normalize the input.** If `data` is `None` or not a string, treat it as empty. This keeps the function total and avoids raising on odd inputs.
2. **Split on whitespace.** `str.split()` with no argument splits on any run of whitespace and drops leading/trailing whitespace, which matches the required behavior for empty or whitespace-only input.
3. **Lowercase each token.** `str.lower()` is applied to each token after splitting. Punctuation is preserved because lowercasing only affects case-sensitive letters.
4. **Count in first-appearance order.** A plain dictionary is used as an ordered counter. Python dictionaries preserve insertion order, so the first time a token is seen it is inserted, and later occurrences only increment its count. This avoids the earlier mistake of sorting alphabetically.
5. **Return the required shape.** Convert the ordered dictionary into a list of two-element lists: `[[token, count], ...]`.

## Why this satisfies the samples

- `"B a B"` splits to `["B", "a", "B"]`, lowercases to `["b", "a", "b"]`, and counts to `{"b": 2, "a": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `b` then `a`, giving `[["b", 2], ["a", 1]]`.
- `" Hi! hi HI! "` splits to `["Hi!", "hi", "HI!"]`, lowercases to `["hi!", "hi", "hi!"]`, and counts to `{"hi!": 2, "hi": 1}`. The first-appearance order is `hi!` then `hi`, giving `[["hi!", 2], ["hi", 1]]`.

## Edge cases

- Empty string `""` → `[]`.
- Whitespace-only string `"   "` → `[]`.
- Single token `"Hello"` → `[["hello", 1]]`.
- Mixed punctuation `"a,a a,a"` → `[["a,a", 2]]` because punctuation is preserved inside tokens.

<hick:project/>

<hick:file path="solution.py">
def solve(data):
    if not isinstance(data, str):
        return []
    tokens = data.split()
    counts = {}
    for token in tokens:
        key = token.lower()
        if key in counts:
            counts[key] += 1
        else:
            counts[key] = 1
    return [[token, count] for token, count in counts.items()]
</hick:file>

</hick:doc></qwenmeasurement20261101:entry>

</qwenmeasurement20261101:doc>
